## WORLD CUP 2030 QUARTERFINAL: ITALY 2–2 ENGLAND AET; ITALY WIN 4–3 ON PENALTIES; SEMIFINALISTS (MANAGER-CONFIRMED)

- **RESULT:** Italy **2–2 England after extra time**, FIFA World Cup 2030 **quarterfinal**; Italy win **4–3 on penalties**, advancing to the **WORLD CUP SEMIFINAL** for the first time since 2006. France is now confirmed by the manager as Italy's next World Cup semifinal opponent. Match date, venue, kickoff and France's preceding results remain unconfirmed and must not be invented. This is a **draw after 120 minutes** with Italy advancing on penalties, not a 3–2 or 6–5 match score.
- **CONFIRMED CHRONOLOGY:** **HALFTIME 0–0**, chances exchanged. **50′ Manuel Locatelli scores for Italy**, assisted by **Francesco Pio Esposito**, after accepting Pio's pass outside the box, beating **two defenders**, then finishing toward the far side. This was **Italy's first opening goal in six matches** at this World Cup (previous five all saw Italy concede first). **77′ Anthony Gordon equalises** for England, 1–1. **Two major Gianluigi Donnarumma saves in second-half stoppage time** keep the game alive; exact minutes of these two saves were NOT provided. ## Porto Champions League final — pre-match manager press conference (manager-confirmed)
- Manager on the historical stakes: “We have a chance to make history. We understand it’s a big deal. But I’m not going to pretend that one final defines the entirety of what we built. Nor will I pretend that winning this match is enough to be considered one of the all time great dynasties.”
- Asked what would qualify the team as an all-time great dynasty: “That’s for pundits, analysts, and football historians to decide. We’re just trying to give them enough to work with to make our case.”
- On selection and Raspadori’s late form: “Every man in that room is capable of being in our starting XI. But I’m not giving away our rotation before the match!”
- On pressure and Porto: “Pressure sits on whoever wants to feel it. We’ve been here before. We know what it takes to win. But let’s not pretend that Porto is here by accident. They’ve slain giants and proven time and time again that the moment isn’t too big. We have a lot of respect for them. But as I’ve always said. Respect, not fear.”
- On imposing Napoli’s game: “By treating them like any other team. City, Bayern, Liverpool. We didn’t wait to see what they wanted to do. We imposed our will. I have no interest in trying a different approach. Stick to our game plan and we can beat anyone.”
- On responding if Napoli go behind: “Because we’ve been there before. We’ve been down and come back in the past. We know what it takes. Why panic now”
- On the goalkeeper decision: “Admittedly, exceedingly so. We have 2 phenomenal keepers. Each with their own massive moments this season. One man getting the nod over the other isn’t a slight on the other. That’s for sure.”
- Final dressing-room message before Porto: “No panic. No fear. Respect the opponent. Play OUR game.”
- No starting XI or goalkeeper selection was revealed.
- Next fixture: Porto, Champions League final, 1 June 2030.

## Management and boundaries
- Manager: **Stefan Saladino** (Napoli and Italy). Assistant manager: **Alessandro “Sandro” Vitale**, a loyal Neapolitan staff member **since day one**, not a new hire. The manager alone controls results, lineups, substitutions, contracts, injuries and time progression.
- Staff-room discussions are **strictly private**. Do not turn internal coaching conversations, jokes, criticism, confidential negotiations, or plans into journalist quotations or public rumours.
- Fictional journalists may analyze publicly visible football, but may not attribute knowledge to a player screenshot or to a user's chat report. No fabricated direct quotes from the manager.
- **Media-team quality warning (manager instruction):** Editorial staff are on thin ice. Publish substantial, differentiated analysis rather than three-paragraph filler. Curate article-specific supporters, rival fans and pundits with real arguments, disagreements and threaded replies. Confirm script load order, live rendering where possible, image quality, and current homepage state before calling work complete.

- Private staff joke about 'invading France' or nationalizing Beier, Olise, Neves is **NOT a public rumour, recruitment plan or actual eligibility change**. Nico Paz's national-team eligibility in the save is not established.

## Continuous event ledger (chronological, manager-confirmed)

### Historical foundations — 2027–28 and 2028–29
- **2027–28:** Napoli won Serie A **and Coppa Italia** under Saladino, completing a domestic double (manager-confirmed). Coppa final score is not established.
- **2028–29:** Napoli retained Serie A, 93 points (28W 9D 1L; 70 scored, 17 conceded). Reached Coppa Italia final, losing to Milan on penalties after 2–2 AET. Won Champions League final **2–0 against Real Madrid**, goals Beier 6' (Pio) and Pio 67' (Beier); Peacock kept clean sheet. Italy reached European Championship final and lost to Germany; Italy had missed three World Cups consecutively.

### Summer 2029 — confirmed business and competitive events
- **Summer window:** Zaïre-Emery, Raspadori and Jetten signed; Hasa recalled. Cucurella, Marianucci, Rao, Giovane and Di Lorenzo departed. Plizzari subsequently joined on a one-year third-choice loan; Meret and Peacock remain ahead of him. Broja deal not completed. Lawton and Burnett remain out on loan.
- **4 Aug:** Napoli 3–0 Real Betis, preseason friendly.
- **15 Aug:** Napoli 1–0 Celtic after extra time, UEFA Super Cup; Davies 118' (Pio assist).
- **26 Aug:** Parma 1–1 Napoli, Serie A MD1; Frigan 24', João Neves stoppage-time equaliser (McTominay assist). Napoli xG 3.1 to Parma 1.3.
- **1 Sep:** Napoli 2–0 Sassuolo, Serie A MD2; Pio 28' (Beier assist), **Moore 88' (Raspadori assist)**. Napoli 16 shots, 7 on target, xG 5.4; Meret clean sheet. League: 4 pts after two matches.

### September 2029 — national-team friendlies and development
- Italy World Cup qualifying is approaching. Staff discussed Project Napotalia: Donnarumma behind a possible Calafiori–Bastoni–Buongiorno–Kayode defence, Tonali and Barella in midfield, Pio and Kean up front, and the future potential of loanee V. Mancini (76 OVR, 94 ceiling). This is **planning, not a confirmed Italy starting XI**.

### Standing update procedure — mandatory
1. Record each newly confirmed event here under its actual in-game date, **append chronologically**, and correct any earlier detail in place if the manager clarifies it.
2. Update the current state above (record, squad, next fixture, injuries, competition status) within **this same file**; distinguish confirmed, proposed, unknown and private.
3. If publishing, update website state and public stories from confirmed facts only; private staff material stays private.
4. Verify the repository update and any affected site integrations. **Never generate CANON-<date>.md or a second active canon.**

- 42′ Scott McTominay scores, Maximilian Beier official assist. FT Manchester City 0–1 Napoli; Napoli advance 2–1 aggregate after 1–1 first leg.
- Possession Napoli **68%**, City **32%**. Manchester City **zero total shots** (not merely zero on target) and **0.0 xG**. Manager described the performance as a defensive masterclass and said Napoli controlled much of possession at 75′.

- Napoli reach third Champions League final in four seasons and have already won 2029–30 Serie A; Coppa Italia final remains pending. Six major trophies confirmed, not seven.
